Write: books/trees/src/paint.py
"""Cole's painter layer over a grown tree: index canvas (material, step), then palette.

Materials: 0 sky/none, 1 foliage, 2 wood, 3 ground.
Foliage steps: 0..3 the sky-lit set (Cole p. 114: planes of a dome, one tone per plane),
               4..6 the sun set (p. 115: "a new and lighter set, quite apart from the others").
Wood steps: 0..4 (cylinder: shadow, core, half, lit, highlight).

Everything is in pixels of a chosen m/px; the tree is projected orthographically (side view,
azimuth yaw), sun from azimuth sun_az (0 = from the left of the picture) and elevation sun_el.
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi


def project(pts, yaw, mpp, ox, oy):
    c, s = np.cos(yaw), np.sin(yaw)
    x = pts[..., 0] * c - pts[..., 1] * s
    depth = pts[..., 0] * s + pts[..., 1] * c          # + toward the viewer
    return ox + x / mpp, oy - pts[..., 2] / mpp, depth


class Canvas:
    def __init__(self, W, H):
        self.W, self.H = W, H
        self.mat = np.zeros((H, W), np.uint8)
        self.step = np.zeros((H, W), np.uint8)


def splat_foliage(tree, W, H, mpp, yaw, ox, oy, sun):
    """Accumulate foliage density, mean depth, and a 'light' value per pixel.

    Each cluster is a small ellipse (pads flatter than tall). Light per cluster = its own
    upward-facing normal . sun, times its unoccluded fraction from an opacity map built by
    slicing clusters along the sun direction (the conifer student's shadow map, simplified).
    """
    F = tree.foliage()
    if not F:
        return None
    P = np.array([f[0] for f in F])
    R = np.array([f[1] for f in F])
    N = np.array([f[2] for f in F])
    plag = np.array([f[3] for f in F])
    x, y, dep = project(P, yaw, mpp, ox, oy)
    # --- occlusion toward the sun: bin clusters in the plane perpendicular to the sun ---
    s = sun / np.linalg.norm(sun)
    a = np.cross(s, [0, 0, 1.0]); a = a / (np.linalg.norm(a) + 1e-9); b = np.cross(s, a)
    u, v, t = P @ a, P @ b, P @ s
    cell = max(0.6, np.median(R) * 1.2)
    iu, iv = np.floor(u / cell).astype(int), np.floor(v / cell).astype(int)
    key = iu * 100003 + iv
    order = np.lexsort((-t, key))           # within a column, highest along sun first
    occl = np.zeros(len(P))
    ks, ts = key[order], t[order]
    run = 0.0
    for j in range(len(order)):
        if j == 0 or ks[j] != ks[j - 1]:
            run = 0.0
        occl[order[j]] = run
        run += R[order[j]] ** 2 / cell ** 2 * 0.5
    transm = np.exp(-occl)
    lam = np.clip(N @ s, 0, 1) * transm
    # --- rasterize ---
    dens = np.zeros((H, W)); lsum = np.zeros((H, W)); dsum = np.zeros((H, W))
    rp = R / mpp
    for i in np.argsort(dep):
        cx, cy, r = x[i], y[i], max(0.5, rp[i])
        ry = r * (0.45 if plag[i] else 0.9)
        x0, x1 = int(np.floor(cx - r)), int(np.ceil(cx + r)) + 1
        y0, y1 = int(np.floor(cy - ry)), int(np.ceil(cy + ry)) + 1
        if x1 < 0 or y1 < 0 or x0 >= W or y0 >= H:
            continue
        xs = np.arange(max(0, x0), min(W, x1)); ys = np.arange(max(0, y0), min(H, y1))
        if len(xs) == 0 or len(ys) == 0:
            continue
        X, Y = np.meshgrid(xs + 0.5, ys + 0.5)
        w = np.clip(1 - ((X - cx) / r) ** 2 - ((Y - cy) / ry) ** 2, 0, None)
        sl = (slice(ys[0], ys[-1] + 1), slice(xs[0], xs[-1] + 1))
        dens[sl] += w
        lsum[sl] += w * lam[i]
        dsum[sl] += w * dep[i]
    L = np.where(dens > 0, lsum / np.maximum(dens, 1e-9), 0)
    D = np.where(dens > 0, dsum / np.maximum(dens, 1e-9), 0)
    return dens, L, D


def raster_wood(tree, W, H, mpp, yaw, ox, oy, sun_x):
    """Wood coverage, depth and a cylinder light coordinate (-1 shadow side .. +1 lit side)."""
    cov = np.zeros((H, W), bool); cyl = np.zeros((H, W)); dep = np.full((H, W), -1e9)
    dead = np.zeros((H, W), bool)
    for p0, p1, r, o, dd in tree.segments():
        (x0, y0, d0), (x1, y1, d1) = [project(np.array(p), yaw, mpp, ox, oy) for p in (p0, p1)]
        rp = r / mpp
        if rp < 0.18:          # thinner than ~1/5 px: invisible (Cole p. 45, 116: twigs a haze)
            continue
        L = max(1e-6, np.hypot(x1 - x0, y1 - y0))
        pad = rp + 1
        xa, xb = int(min(x0, x1) - pad), int(max(x0, x1) + pad) + 1
        ya, yb = int(min(y0, y1) - pad), int(max(y0, y1) + pad) + 1
        xa, ya, xb, yb = max(0, xa), max(0, ya), min(W, xb), min(H, yb)
        if xa >= xb or ya >= yb:
            continue
        X, Y = np.meshgrid(np.arange(xa, xb) + 0.5, np.arange(ya, yb) + 0.5)
        t = np.clip(((X - x0) * (x1 - x0) + (Y - y0) * (y1 - y0)) / L ** 2, 0, 1)
        px, py = x0 + t * (x1 - x0), y0 + t * (y1 - y0)
        dx, dy = X - px, Y - py
        dist = np.hypot(dx, dy)
        rr = max(rp, 0.5)
        m = dist <= rr
        # sub-pixel twigs: draw only if they cover at least ~half a pixel along their core
        if rp < 0.5:
            m = dist <= 0.5
        nx, ny = -(y1 - y0) / L, (x1 - x0) / L       # normal in screen
        side = (dx * nx + dy * ny) / rr
        side = side * np.sign(nx * sun_x + 1e-9)     # + toward the sun
        dz = d0 + t * (d1 - d0)
        sl = (slice(ya, yb), slice(xa, xb))
        upd = m & (dz > dep[sl])
        cov[sl] |= m
        cyl[sl] = np.where(upd, side, cyl[sl])
        dep[sl] = np.where(upd, dz, dep[sl])
        dead[sl] |= m & dd
    return cov, cyl, dep, dead
Write: books/trees/src/cole.py
"""Cole's rules as pixel operations. Each rule is a function with its page reference and a switch,
so a sheet can show the tree with and without it (the ablation is the exercise).

Pipeline (render()):
  grow (arch.py) -> splat foliage + raster wood (paint.py)
  -> R1 mass: coverage from density, one foliage mass            (Cole pp. 51, 80)
  -> R2 outline of straight pieces                               (pp. 82-94, fig. 63-66)
  -> R3 dome planes under sky light: one tone per plane          (p. 114, fig. 95)
  -> R4 sun set: a separate lighter family, only where lit        (pp. 114-115)
  -> R5 sky holes: colourless rims, globes, veiled holes darker   (pp. 69-81)
  -> R6 wood: telescope trunk, thin boughs lose modelling in light (pp. 115, 193, 45)
  -> palette
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from paint import splat_foliage, raster_wood

FOL, WOOD, GROUND, SKY = 1, 2, 3, 0
RIM = 7          # foliage step used for the colourless hole/edge rim (a greyed mid tone)


def rules(**kw):
    r = dict(straight=True, dome=True, sunset=True, holes=True, rim=True, fringe=True,
             thin_in_light=True, straight_tol=1.2, holes_min=2, veil=True)
    r.update(kw)
    return r


def crown_mask(dens, thr):
    return dens > thr


def straighten(mask, tol, keep_small=True):
    """R2. Rebuild the silhouette from straight pieces: trace each outer contour, simplify with
    Douglas-Peucker at `tol` px, refill. Cole p. 92: 'The outline of a tree is made up of many
    straight lines ... the comparative length of these straight pieces determine the character of
    the curve.' Holes (sky apertures) are kept from the original mask."""
    from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
    try:
        import cv2
    except ImportError:
        cv2 = None
    H, W = mask.shape
    if cv2 is None:
        return mask
    m8 = mask.astype(np.uint8)
    cs, hier = cv2.findContours(m8, cv2.RETR_CCOMP, cv2.CHAIN_APPROX_NONE)
    out = np.zeros_like(m8)
    for k, c in enumerate(cs):
        if hier[0][k][3] != -1:
            continue                 # holes handled separately
        if len(c) < 8:
            cv2.drawContours(out, [c], -1, 1, -1)
            continue
        ap = cv2.approxPolyDP(c, tol, True)
        cv2.fillPoly(out, [ap], 1)
    # holes from the original survive (they are drawn by R5)
    holes = ndi.binary_fill_holes(mask) & ~mask
    out = out.astype(bool) & ~holes
    return out


def dome_steps(mask, n=4, sun_x=-1.0):
    """R3. The crown as planes of a dome under a grey sky (Cole fig. 95): normal from the smoothed
    mass, tone by how much the plane faces the sky, quantized into n steps (0 dark .. n-1 light).
    Returns float coordinate 0..n-1."""
    if not mask.any():
        return np.zeros(mask.shape)
    dist = ndi.distance_transform_edt(mask)
    scale = max(2.0, np.percentile(dist[mask], 95))
    h = np.sqrt(np.clip(dist / scale, 0, 1))           # dome height from edge distance
    hs = ndi.gaussian_filter(h, max(1.0, scale * 0.25))
    gy, gx = np.gradient(hs)
    nz = 1.0 / np.sqrt(1 + (gx * scale) ** 2 + (gy * scale) ** 2)
    ny = -gy * scale * nz                               # + = facing up
    sky = 0.55 * nz + 0.45 * np.clip(ny, -1, 1)        # grey sky: up-facing planes lightest
    sky = sky + 0.12 * np.clip(-gx * scale * nz * np.sign(sun_x), -1, 1)
    v = (sky - sky[mask].min()) / (np.ptp(sky[mask]) + 1e-9)
    return v * (n - 1)


def render(tree, mpp, W, H, ox=None, oy=None, yaw=0.0, sun_az=0.0, sun_el=35.0, R=None,
           dens_thr=0.35):
    R = R or rules()
    ox = W / 2 if ox is None else ox
    oy = H - 2 if oy is None else oy
    az, el = np.radians(sun_az), np.radians(sun_el)
    # sun vector: az 0 -> light comes from the left of the picture (sun at -x, toward viewer a bit)
    sun = np.array([-np.cos(az) * np.cos(el), -np.sin(az) * np.cos(el), np.sin(el)])
    sun_x = -np.cos(az)
    mat = np.zeros((H, W), np.uint8); step = np.zeros((H, W), np.uint8)
    # wood
    cov, cyl, wdep, dead = raster_wood(tree, W, H, mpp, yaw, ox, oy, sun_x)
    ws = np.clip(np.round((cyl + 1) / 2 * 3 + 0.3), 0, 4).astype(np.uint8)
    mat[cov] = WOOD; step[cov] = ws[cov]
    sp = splat_foliage(tree, W, H, mpp, yaw, ox, oy, sun)
    if sp is None:
        return mat, step, {}
    dens, L, D = sp
    m = crown_mask(dens, dens_thr)
    raw = m.copy()
    if R['straight']:
        m = straighten(m, R['straight_tol'])
    # R3 dome / R4 sun
    if R['dome']:
        base = dome_steps(m, 4, sun_x)
    else:
        base = np.full(m.shape, 1.5)
    fs = np.clip(np.round(base), 0, 3).astype(np.uint8)
    if R['sunset']:
        lit = (L > np.quantile(L[m], 0.55)) & m if m.any() else m
        # sun marks only on the sun-facing upper part of each clump: pixel above not denser
        sun_step = np.where(L > np.quantile(L[m], 0.85) if m.any() else False, 6, 5).astype(np.uint8)
        fs = np.where(lit, np.maximum(sun_step - 1 + (base > 2), 4), fs).astype(np.uint8)
    # wood in front of foliage stays; foliage in front of wood covers it (depth test)
    front = m & ~(cov & (wdep > D + 0.3))
    mat[front] = FOL; step[front] = fs[front]
    info = dict(raw=raw, mask=m, dens=dens, L=L)
    # R5 holes
    if R['holes']:
        holes = ndi.binary_fill_holes(m) & ~m
        lab, n = ndi.label(holes)
        sizes = ndi.sum(holes, lab, range(1, n + 1))
        for k, sz in enumerate(sizes, 1):
            hk = lab == k
            if sz < R['holes_min']:
                mat[hk & ~cov] = FOL; step[hk & ~cov] = 1   # tiny pinholes close up
                continue
            if R['veil']:
                # veiled holes (twigs/leaves in them) are darker than open sky (p. 71)
                vd = dens[hk].mean() / max(dens_thr, 1e-9)
                if vd > 0.15:
                    mat[hk & (mat == SKY)] = SKY
                    step[hk & (mat == SKY)] = 1                  # sky step 1 = veiled sky
        if R['rim']:
            # rims of holes are colourless: foliage pixels touching a hole take the rim tone
            ring = ndi.binary_dilation(holes, iterations=1) & m & (mat == FOL)
            step[ring & (step < 4)] = RIM
    if R['rim']:
        # the outer edge 'less coloured than the bulk' (p. 71): outline pixels facing sky
        edge = m & ~ndi.binary_erosion(m) & (mat == FOL)
        step[edge & (step < 4) & (step > 0)] = RIM
    info['holes'] = ndi.binary_fill_holes(m) & ~m if R['holes'] else None
    return mat, step, info


def palette_default():
    """Morning, westside conifer. Foliage 0-3 sky set (blue-black to grey-green), 4-6 sun set
    (brown-olive to warm tip), 7 rim (a greyed mid, between foliage and sky)."""
    fol = [(14, 24, 22), (26, 42, 36), (40, 60, 48), (58, 80, 62),
           (92, 94, 44), (128, 126, 58), (170, 160, 84), (84, 104, 104)]
    wood = [(30, 22, 20), (52, 38, 32), (82, 62, 50), (118, 92, 72), (150, 122, 96)]
    return dict(fol=fol, wood=wood, sky=[(150, 196, 230), (118, 158, 186)], ground=[(60, 50, 40)])


def to_rgb(mat, step, pal, sky_grad=True):
    H, W = mat.shape
    out = np.zeros((H, W, 3), np.uint8)
    if sky_grad:
        top, bot = np.array((110, 160, 214)), np.array((188, 214, 232))
        t = np.linspace(0, 1, H)[:, None]
        g = (top * (1 - t) + bot * t)
        out[:] = np.round(g[:, None, :]).astype(np.uint8)
    veiled = (mat == SKY) & (step == 1)
    out[veiled] = (np.array(out[veiled], float) * 0.82).astype(np.uint8)
    for k, c in enumerate(pal['fol']):
        out[(mat == FOL) & (step == k)] = c
    for k, c in enumerate(pal['wood']):
        out[(mat == WOOD) & (step == k)] = c
    return out
Bash: python3 -c "import cv2; print(cv2.__version__)"
python3 -c "import cv2; print(cv2.__version__)"

Write: books/trees/src/species_models.py
"""Washington species as Hallé models with Cole's habits. Assignments marked [mine] are my
reading; the book assigns only the genera noted.

Douglas-fir   Massart-like [mine]: rhythmic leader with whorls + interwhorl laterals, plagiotropic
              near-level boughs with upswept tips; clear bole in the forest; old: reiterated
              upper limbs and broken top (Van Pelt; naturalist 05-old-growth).
W. hemlock    Troll/Mangenot (Hallé p. 246, 329: Tsuga's drooping leader); drooping boughs,
              dorsiventral sprays; shade tolerant: deep crown.
W. redcedar   Massart-like with J-limbs [mine]: boughs droop then turn up (Cole's 'steel spring',
              fig. 87), pendulous sprays; old: many reiterated leaders (candelabra), dead spike.
Sitka spruce  Massart (Hallé: Picea): stiff level boughs, upturned tips, pendulous branchlets.
Subalpine fir Massart (Hallé: Abies; krummholz p. 199): narrow spire, short boughs.
Red alder     Rauh [mine] (Cole: 3-ranked buds, boughs evenly spaced, 'identified by angle').
Bigleaf maple Rauh (Hallé: Acer) with forks from terminal flowering (Cole figs. 130-131).
Garry oak     Rauh (Hallé: Quercus) 'supple': leaders replaced, sinuous; right-angled boughs (Cole).
Madrone       Scarrone [mine]: sympodial through terminal panicles, leaning, few big limbs.
"""
from arch import Species

SP = {
    'douglas': Species('douglas-fir', model='massart', height=70, h_rate=0.9, h_tau=110,
                       tier=(3, 5), inter=2, angle=84, angle_top=60, lat_rate=0.42, lat_tau=30,
                       plagio=True, flatten=0.03, sag=0.10, tip_up=0.012, sub_n=0, max_order=1,
                       shade_prune=0.55, open_prune=0.06, stub_keep=0.5, needle_years=7,
                       cluster=1.1, reach=1.2, reiter_age=220, reiter_rate=0.02, reiter_max=8,
                       top_break=0.004, trunk_k=0.013),
    'hemlock': Species('western hemlock', model='troll', height=55, h_rate=0.7, h_tau=110,
                       tier=(2, 4), inter=3, angle=70, angle_top=55, lat_rate=0.40, lat_tau=28,
                       plagio=True, flatten=0.02, sag=0.35, tip_up=0.0, sub_n=0, max_order=1,
                       nod=1.4, nod_years=2, shade_prune=0.30, open_prune=0.03, needle_years=5,
                       cluster=0.9, needle=None) if False else
               Species('western hemlock', model='troll', height=55, h_rate=0.7, h_tau=110,

s=s[:a]+'''    def foliage(self):
        """Clusters: (pos, r, normal, plagio, dir).
        Leaders carry only this year's tuft. Branches carry foliage on nodes younger than
        spray_years: on a plagiotropic bough each node bears a flat fan of branchlets (Cole
        figs. 108-110: 'the lowest branchlets ranged in something like one flat plane'),
        widest a few years back from the tip and narrowing to the tip, so each bough ends in a
        wedge. The fan is splatted as clusters offset to either side in the bough's plane."""
        sp, out = self.sp, []
        sy = sp.spray_years or sp.needle_years
        for i, a in enumerate(self.axes):
            if a.dead or len(a.pts) < 2:
                continue
            ages = a.age_nodes
            pts = self.nodded(i, a.pts)
            for k in range(1, len(pts)):
                age = self.year - ages[k] if k < len(ages) else 0
                if a.order == 0 or a.reiterated:
                    if age > 1:
                        continue
                    out.append((pts[k], sp.cluster * 0.6, UP, False, UP))
                    continue
                if age > sy:
                    continue
                d = _norm(pts[k] - pts[k - 1])
                r = sp.cluster * (0.75 ** max(0, a.order - 1))
                if a.kind == 'plagio' and sp.pad:
                    w = sp.reach * min(1.0, (age + 1) / 6.0)       # fan half-width grows back from tip
                    side = _norm(np.cross(d, UP))
                    nrm = _norm(UP - 0.4 * d[2] * d)
                    out.append((pts[k], r, nrm, True, d))
                    if w > 0.2:
                        for sgn in (-1, 1):
                            q = pts[k] + sgn * w * side - UP * sp.fan_droop * w
                            out.append((q, r * 0.85, nrm, True, d))
                else:
                    out.append((pts[k], r, UP, False, d))
        return out
'''

open('src/arch.py','w').write(s)

"""Shared helpers: cached growth, sheet assembly, 8x zoom."""
import os, pickle, sys
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))
from arch import Tree
from species_models import SP

ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
CACHE = os.path.join(ROOT, 'tmp', 'cache')
os.makedirs(CACHE, exist_ok=True)


def grow(name, age, seed=0, open_grown=False, **over):
    import dataclasses
    sp = SP[name]
    if over:
        sp = dataclasses.replace(sp, **over)
    key = f"{name}_{age}_{seed}_{int(open_grown)}_" + "_".join(f"{k}{v}" for k, v in sorted(over.items()))
    f = os.path.join(CACHE, key.replace('/', '') + '.pkl')
    if os.path.exists(f):
        return pickle.load(open(f, 'rb'))
    t = Tree(sp, seed=seed, open_grown=open_grown)
    t.nod_az = 0.6
    t.grow(age)
    pickle.dump(t, open(f, 'wb'))
    return t


def up(im, k):
    im = im if isinstance(im, Image.Image) else Image.fromarray(im)
    return im.resize((im.size[0] * k, im.size[1] * k), Image.NEAREST)


def sheet(cells, cols, pad=6, labels=None, title=None, bg=(245, 245, 240)):
    """cells: list of PIL images (any size); arranged in a grid with labels under each."""
    cells = [c if isinstance(c, Image.Image) else Image.fromarray(c) for c in cells]
    cw = max(c.size[0] for c in cells); ch = max(c.size[1] for c in cells)
    rows = (len(cells) + cols - 1) // cols
    th = 18 if title else 0
    S = Image.new('RGB', (cols * (cw + pad) + pad, th + rows * (ch + pad + 14) + pad), bg)
    d = ImageDraw.Draw(S)
    if title:
        d.text((pad, 3), title, fill=(0, 0, 0))
    for i, c in enumerate(cells):
        x = pad + (i % cols) * (cw + pad); y = th + pad + (i // cols) * (ch + pad + 14)
        S.paste(c.convert('RGB'), (x, y))
        if labels:
            d.text((x, y + c.size[1] + 1), labels[i], fill=(0, 0, 0))
    return S


def photo(path, h):
    im = Image.open(path).convert('RGB')
    w = int(im.size[0] * h / im.size[1])
    return im.resize((w, h), Image.LANCZOS)

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat >> arch.py <<'E'


def compact(tree):
    """Drop dead single-point axes (remapping parent indices)."""
    keep = [i for i, a in enumerate(tree.axes) if not (a.dead and len(a.pts) <= 1)]
    if len(keep) == len(tree.axes):
        return tree
    remap = {o: n for n, o in enumerate(keep)}
    axes, dirs = [], {}
    for o in keep:
        a = tree.axes[o]
        a.parent = remap.get(a.parent, -1) if a.parent >= 0 else -1
        axes.append(a)
        dirs[remap[o]] = tree.dir.get(o, UP)
    tree.axes, tree.dir = axes, dirs
    return tree

# Copying Ferrari passage by passage

*By the master-copy student. The story of how I learned this, dead ends included, is in [POST.md](POST.md); my raw working thread is [the notebook](../../notebooks/master-copy/notebook.md).*

I learned to paint in Mark Ferrari's pixel language by copying him: I picked passages of his tarn painting and wrote a small program to rebuild each one, compared at 3× and 8× against the real thing, until it held up. Those copies were the best work I did, better than the full painter I built from them afterwards. So this is what I teach: **how to study a master's passages, and the passage recipes I got out of it.** Each recipe shows his passage beside my copy, the real code from my study that made it, what I measured to get there, and the traps, each with the bad image I actually made.

The code lives in my study folder, `~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/master-copy/src/`: one file per copy (`c1_sky_rock.py` … `c5_boulder.py`), the shared toolkit `fl.py`, and the full painter `tarn.py`.

![his island and my copy](howto/c2_whole.png)

*The grass island: Ferrari (left), my copy (right), at 4×.*

---

## Method

1. **[Read the master's own files](howto/01-read-the-masters-files.md).** Find the native pixel grid, get his original indexed pictures, align your reference to them, and read a passage as text. The key finding: in his colour-cycling scenes the morning and afternoon versions share one index map, so **the index map is the form and the palette is the light.** Paint classes, not colours. (My own mound copy relights with his morning palette exactly as his does.)

   ![relight](howto/relight_mine.png)

2. **[Compare obsessively, and measure the texture](howto/02-compare-and-measure.md).** A fixed 3×/8× comparison sheet; blurLab, colour histogram and run lengths; and the cheap statistics that found what my eye couldn't: class shares by region, dither structure against random, which class caps a run, and which class sits beside which, against its base rate.

## Passages

3. **[The banded dusk sky](howto/03-banded-sky.md).** 11 near-isovalue steps, flat bands, 2×2 checker seams.

   ![sky](howto/c1_sky8.png)

4. **[A sunlit rock ridge](howto/04-lit-ridge-with-echo.md).** A broken-checker violet mass, a hot rim only on sun-facing crests, the echo line 2–3 px inside, clean stair-step slopes, and snow that borrows the sky's colours.

   ![ridge](howto/c1_ridge8.png)

5. **[Pines from their anatomy](howto/05-pines.md).** A 1-px spire, tiers every 4–5 rows, 20% holes, lit ochre on the sun-side tips; read character by character off his pixels.

   ![pines](howto/c1_pine6.png)

6. **[The four-class mound with a fringed crest](howto/06-four-class-mound.md).** Reflected light | core shadow | halftone | lit, scattered so no zone is pure, checker-biased in shade and clumped in light, and a crest that's a fringe, not a cut.

   ![crest](howto/c2_crest8.png)

7. **[The sunlit bank](howto/

Write: books/trees/src/px.py
"""A small hand-drawing kit for deliberate pixel placement.

Everything drawn here is placed by coordinates or ASCII rows I write while looking at the
book's figure (and its box-downsampled guide). No procedural geometry.

Canvas holds palette indices. ASCII stamping: each character maps to an index ('.' or ' ' = leave).
"""
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image


class Canvas:
    def __init__(self, w, h, bg=0):
        self.a = np.full((h, w), bg, np.uint8)
        self.w, self.h = w, h

    def px(self, x, y, c):
        if 0 <= x < self.w and 0 <= y < self.h:
            self.a[y, x] = c

    def hrun(self, x, y, n, c):
        for i in range(n):
            self.px(x + i, y, c)

    def vrun(self, x, y, n, c):
        for i in range(n):
            self.px(x, y + i, c)

    def steps(self, x, y, runs, c, dx=1, dy=-1, vertical=False):
        """A clean-run line: runs = list of run lengths. Horizontal runs stepping dy each run
        (or vertical runs stepping dx if vertical). Returns end point."""
        for n in runs:
            for i in range(n):
                self.px(x, y, c)
                if vertical:
                    y += dy if dy else 1
                else:
                    x += dx
            if vertical:
                x += dx
            else:
                y += dy
        return x, y

    def stamp(self, rows, x0, y0, cmap):
        for j, row in enumerate(rows):
            for i, ch in enumerate(row):
                if ch in cmap:
                    self.px(x0 + i, y0 + j, cmap[ch])

    def poly(self, pts, c):
        from PIL import ImageDraw
        m = Image.new('L', (self.w, self.h), 0)
        ImageDraw.Draw(m).polygon(pts, fill=1)
        self.a[np.array(m, bool)] = c

    def img(self, pal, k=1):
        rgb = np.array(pal, np.uint8)[self.a]
        im = Image.fromarray(rgb)
        return im.resize((self.w * k, self.h * k), Image.NEAREST) if k > 1 else im


def guide(path, w, h, levels=" .:-=+*#%@", crop=None, invert=True, show=True):
    """Box-downsample a reference to w x h and print it as characters (dark = dense)."""
    im = Image.open(path).convert('L')
    if crop:
        im = im.crop(crop)
    g = np.array(im.resize((w, h), Image.BOX), float) / 255.0
    if invert:
        g = 1 - g
    g = (g - g.min()) / (np.ptp(g) + 1e-9)
    idx = np.clip((g * (len(levels) - 1)).round().astype(int), 0, len(levels) - 1)
    rows = ["".join(levels[i] for i in r) for r in idx]
    if show:
        print("     " + "".join(str((i // 10) % 10) for i in range(w)))
        print("     " + "".join(str(i % 10) for i in range(w)))
        for j, r in enumerate(rows):
            print(f"{j:3d}  {r}")
    return g

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat > copy_halle.py <<'E'
"""Master copies of Hallé's key figures (pp. 92-97), drawn pixel by pixel."""
from px import Canvas, guide
from lib import sheet, up
from PIL import Image
PAPER, INK, LEAF = 0, 1, 2
PAL = [(236, 232, 220), (30, 28, 26), (96, 92, 84)]


def leaves_below(c, pts, every=2):
    """Hallé's hanging leaf loops: a 2-px drop under every other branch pixel."""
    for i, (x, y) in enumerate(pts):
        if i % every == 0:
            c.px(x, y + 1, LEAF); c.px(x, y + 2, LEAF)


def path(c, x, y, moves, col=INK):
    """moves: string of steps; 'r','l' = 1 px horizontal, 'u','d' vertical, 'R','L' diagonal up
    (R = up-right, L = up-left), 'r3' style repeats. Returns list of pixels drawn."""
    import re
    out = [(x, y)]
    c.px(x, y, col)
    for m, n in re.findall(r'([rludRLFB])(\d*)', moves):
        for _ in range(int(n or 1)):
            dx, dy = dict(r=(1, 0), l=(-1, 0), u=(0, -1), d=(0, 1), R=(1, -1), L=(-1, -1),
                          F=(1, 1), B=(-1, 1))[m]
            x, y = x + dx, y + dy
            c.px(x, y, col); out.append((x, y))
    return out


def massart_big(c, x0, y0):
    """Largest stage of Massart's key figure (p. 92). Trunk at x0, ground at y0.
    Tiers at 4 levels; branches plagiotropic: rise gently from the trunk, run level, the lower
    (older) ones droop at the tip; leaves hang as loops under the outer two thirds."""
    X = x0
    # trunk: 2 px wide below the 2nd tier, 1 px above; root peg below the ground
    c.vrun(X, y0 - 58, 58, INK); c.vrun(X + 1, y0 - 38, 38, INK)
    c.vrun(X, y0 + 1, 4, INK)
    c.hrun(X - 31, y0, 64, INK)
    # leader tip: small paired leaves (spiral phyllotaxis on the orthotropic axis)
    for yy in range(y0 - 57, y0 - 2, 4):
        c.px(X - 1, yy, LEAF); c.px(X + 2 if yy > y0 - 38 else X + 1, yy + 2, LEAF)
    # tier 1 (youngest, y0-54): short, rising
    L = path(c, X - 1, y0 - 53, "l2L1l2L1l3")
    R = path(c, X + 1, y0 - 53, "r2R1r2R1r3")
    leaves_below(c, L[3:]); leaves_below(c, R[3:])
    # tier 2 (y0-38): long, rising to the outside, tip level
    L = path(c, X - 1, y0 - 38, "l3L1l3L1l3L1l4L1l6")
    R = path(c, X + 2, y0 - 38, "r3R1r3R1r3R1r4R1r6")
    leaves_below(c, L[6:]); leaves_below(c, R[6:])
    # tier 3 (y0-27): near level, long; outer third droops a little then the tip turns up
    L = path(c, X - 1, y0 - 27, "l5L1l8L1l10B1l4L1")
    R = path(c, X + 2, y0 - 27, "r5R1r8R1r10F1r4R1")
    leaves_below(c, L[5:]); leaves_below(c, R[5:])
    # tier 4 (oldest, y0-13): longest; level then pendulous with age
    L = path(c, X - 1, y0 - 13, "l4L1l9L1l10B1l3B1l2")
    R = path(c, X + 2, y0 - 13, "r4R1r9R1r10F1r3F1r2")
    leaves_below(c, L[5:]); leaves_below(c, R[5:])


def massart_small(c, x0, y0, h, tiers, spans):
    c.vrun(x0, y0 - h, h, INK); c.vrun(x0, y0 + 1, 2, INK)
    for (dy, sp) in zip(tiers, spans):
        L = path(c, x0 - 1, y0 - dy, "l%dL1l%d" % (sp // 2, sp - sp // 2))
        R = path(c, x0 + 1, y0 - dy, "r%dR1r%d" % (sp // 2, sp - sp // 2))
        leaves_below(c, L[2:]); leaves_below(c, R[2:])
    for yy in range(y0 - h + 1, y0 - 1, 4):
        c.px(x0 - 1, yy, LEAF)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    c = Canvas(190, 72)
    g = 66
    massart_small(c, 8, g, 10, [7], [4])
    massart_small(c, 30, g, 20, [16, 7], [4, 8])
    massart_small(c, 66, g, 34, [30, 19, 8], [5, 13, 16])
    massart_big(c, 140, g)
    c.hrun(0, g, 190, INK)
    ref = Image.open('../copies/ref_massart.png').convert('RGB').resize((190 * 3, int(280 * 190 * 3 / 510)))
    S = sheet([ref, c.img(PAL, 3)], 1, labels=['Hallé p. 92, Massart (book)', 'pixel copy, 190x72, shown 3x'])
    S.save('../copies/massart_v1.png')
E

Bash: cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat >> copy_halle.py <<'E' def troll_big(c, X, g): """Largest stage of Troll's key figure (p. 97, monopodial trunk). Every axis is
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat >> copy_halle.py <<'E'


def troll_big(c, X, g):
    """Largest stage of Troll's key figure (p. 97, monopodial trunk). Every axis is plagiotropic:
    it arches up out of the trunk, crests, and falls; leaves hang only from the arch. The trunk is
    the erected base of the relay axes; the current leader is itself an arch (top)."""
    c.vrun(X, g - 49, 49, INK); c.vrun(X + 1, g - 30, 30, INK); c.vrun(X, g + 1, 3, INK)
    # the leader arch: up, then over to the right (nodding tip)
    P = arc(c, X, g - 50, 1, [1, 1, 2, 3, 4, 3, 2, 1, 1, 1], [-1, -1, -1, -1, 0, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1])
    for x, y in P[1:2]: pass
    c.vrun(X, g - 52, 3, INK)
    leaves_below(c, P[4:])
    # arches: (height above ground, side, runs, dys) -- chosen off the guide
    specs = [
        (49, -1, [1, 2, 3, 4, 3, 2, 1], [-1, -1, -1, 0, 1, 1, 1]),
        (48, 1, [1, 2, 3, 5, 4, 2, 1, 1], [-1, -1, 0, 0, 1, 1, 1, 1]),
        (31, -1, [1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 4, 3, 2], [-1, -1, -1, -1, 0, 1, 1, 1]),
        (30, 1, [1, 2, 3, 5, 5, 3, 2], [-1, -1, -1, 0, 1, 1, 1]),
        (13, -1, [1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 3, 2, 1], [-1, -1, -1, 0, 0, 1, 1, 1]),
        (13, 1, [1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 4, 2, 1], [-1, -1, -1, 0, 0, 1, 1, 1]),
    ]
    for h, d, runs, dys in specs:
        P = arc(c, X + (2 if d > 0 else -1), g - h, d, runs, dys)
        leaves_below(c, P[3:])


def troll_small(c, X, g, h, specs):
    c.vrun(X, g - h, h, INK); c.vrun(X, g + 1, 2, INK)
    P = arc(c, X, g - h - 1, 1, [1, 1, 2, 2, 1, 1], [-1, -1, 0, 1, 1, 1])
    leaves_below(c, P[2:])
    for hh, d, runs, dys in specs:
        P = arc(c, X + (1 if d > 0 else -1), g - hh, d, runs, dys)
        leaves_below(c, P[2:])


def troll_sheet():
    c = Canvas(132, 70)
    g = 66
    troll_small(c, 10, g, 3, [])
    troll_small(c, 30, g, 12, [(12, -1, [1, 2, 3, 2, 1], [-1, -1, 0, 1, 1]), (6, 1, [1, 2, 3, 2], [-1, 0, 1, 1])])
    troll_small(c, 58, g, 24, [(24, -1, [1, 2, 3, 3, 2, 1], [-1, -1, 0, 1, 1, 1]),
                               (18, 1, [1, 2, 3, 3, 2], [-1, -1, 0, 1, 1]),
                               (8, -1, [1, 2, 4, 3, 2], [-1, -1, 0, 1, 1]),
                               (7, 1, [1, 2, 4, 3, 2], [-1, -1, 0, 1, 1])])
    troll_big(c, 100, g)
    c.hrun(0, g, 132, INK)
    ref = Image.open('../copies/ref_troll.png').convert('RGB')
    ref = ref.resize((int(ref.size[0] * 0.25 * 3), int(ref.size[1] * 0.25 * 3)))
    sheet([ref, c.img(PAL, 3)], 1, labels=['Hallé p. 97, Troll (book), same scale', 'pixel copy 132x70, 3x']).save('../copies/troll_v1.png')


troll_sheet()

Bash: cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat > copy_fig40.py <<'E' """Master copy of Cole fig. 40 (p. 79): the sky apertures of a tree, as pixels. Underpainting from the box-dow
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat > copy_fig40.py <<'E'
"""Master copy of Cole fig. 40 (p. 79): the sky apertures of a tree, as pixels.
Underpainting from the box-downsampled plate, then my decisions, each from Cole's text:

  - the outline cut in straight pieces (p. 92, fig. 41's polygon)
  - holes: pinholes closed; each hole gets a colourless rim (p. 71 rule 1), the open ones the
    sky's light in the centre (rule 2), veiled ones a darker sky (rule 3)
  - the limbs: lit bands against the dark mass, lost (thinned) where they cross open sky
    (p. 45 'slender forms seen against the light appear more slender').
"""
import numpy as np, cv2
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from PIL import Image
from px import guide, Canvas
from lib import sheet

SKY, VEIL, RIM, DARK, MID, WOODL, WOODD, HILL, HILLD = range(9)
PAL = [(190, 200, 204), (160, 170, 176), (120, 128, 124), (44, 52, 50), (70, 78, 72),
       (176, 172, 158), (96, 92, 84), (98, 100, 96), (80, 82, 80)]

g = guide('../copies/ref_fig40.png', 60, 72, show=False)
v = np.clip((g * 10).astype(int), 0, 9)
H, W = v.shape
yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
hill = ((v >= 3) & (((yy >= 40) & (xx >= 40)) | (yy >= 62) | ((xx >= 55) & (yy >= 19))))
crown = (v >= 5) & (yy < 48) & ~hill
# naive quantization (for the comparison panel)
naive = np.where(hill, HILL, np.where(crown, DARK, np.where(v >= 3, MID, SKY))).astype(np.uint8)

# --- my copy ---
a = np.full((H, W), SKY, np.uint8)
a[hill] = HILL
# 1. crown outline in straight pieces
m = ndi.binary_closing(crown, iterations=1)
cs, hier = cv2.findContours(m.astype(np.uint8), cv2.RETR_CCOMP, cv2.CHAIN_APPROX_NONE)
out = np.zeros((H, W), np.uint8)
for k, c in enumerate(cs):
    if hier[0][k][3] == -1 and len(c) > 6:
        cv2.fillPoly(out, [cv2.approxPolyDP(c, 1.6, True)], 1)
cm = out.astype(bool)
holes = cm & ~ndi.binary_opening(crown | ~cm, iterations=0) if False else cm & (v <= 4)
lab, n = ndi.label(holes)
keep = np.zeros_like(holes)
for k in range(1, n + 1):
    h = lab == k
    if h.sum() >= 3:                     # pinholes close up: the mass keeps its bulk
        keep |= h
a[cm] = DARK
# texture inside the mass: the plate's lighter hatching read as a sparse mid, on the left/top
a[cm & (v == 6) & (xx < 40)] = MID
# 2. holes: open (value <=2) = sky; veiled (3-4) = veiled sky; 1-px colourless rim around each
for k in range(1, ndi.label(keep)[1] + 1):
    pass
a[keep & (v <= 2)] = SKY
a[keep & (v >= 3)] = VEIL
rim = ndi.binary_dilation(keep, iterations=1) & cm & ~keep
a[rim] = RIM
# outer edge against sky: a broken colourless rim on the upper edges only (less coloured edge)
edge = cm & ~ndi.binary_erosion(cm) & (a == DARK)
up_open = np.roll(~cm, 1, axis=0)
a[edge & up_open & ((xx + yy) % 3 != 0)] = RIM
# 3. limbs: the pale wood of the plate (low values inside the crown's hull, below the mass)
hull = ndi.binary_fill_holes(cm) | ndi.binary_closing(cm, iterations=6)
wood = (v <= 3) & (yy >= 14) & ~keep
# the plate's limbs are pale strokes; take the guide's pale thin features inside the hull + trunk
trunk = np.zeros_like(wood)
for y in range(40, H):
    # trunk: the pale band between crown/hill, from the guide rows (value 0-2 run near x 36-46)
    row = v[y]
    xs = [x for x in range(30, 50) if row[x] <= 2 and hill[y, min(W - 1, x + 3)]]
    for x in xs[-4:]:
        trunk[y, x] = True
limb = (wood & hull & ndi.binary_dilation(cm, iterations=2)) | trunk
a[limb & (a != SKY)] = WOODL
# shadow side of each limb (sun from the left): right-hand edge pixel dark
sh = limb & ~np.roll(limb, -1, axis=1)
a[sh & (a == WOODL)] = WOODD

def img(arr, k=4):
    return Image.fromarray(np.array(PAL, np.uint8)[arr]).resize((W * k, H * k), Image.NEAREST)

ref = Image.open('../copies/ref_fig40.png').convert('RGB').resize((W * 4, H * 4))
sheet([ref, img(naive), img(a)], 3, labels=['Cole fig. 40 (plate)', 'naive: plate quantized', 'copy: Cole\'s rules']).save('../copies/fig40_v1.png')
np.save('../copies/fig40_v1.npy', a)

Bash: cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat > copy_fig40b.py <<'E' """Master copy of Cole fig. 40 (p. 79), drawn by hand at 44x54. Every mass is a polygon whose vertices I chos
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat > copy_fig40b.py <<'E'
"""Master copy of Cole fig. 40 (p. 79), drawn by hand at 44x54.
Every mass is a polygon whose vertices I chose off the plate (the outline in straight pieces,
fig. 41 / p. 92); every hole, limb and rim pixel is placed. Cole's rules applied by hand:
 p. 71 (1) hole rims colourless  (2) small holes are globes, lightest centre  (3) veiled holes
 darker than open sky  (4) local colour strongest in the middle of the big masses
 p. 80 holes linked in chains by strong edges; p. 45 limbs thin where they cross bright sky."""
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from px import Canvas
from lib import sheet

SKY, VEIL, RIM, DARK, CORE, LIT, WOOD, WOODD, HILL, HILLD, SKYC = range(11)
PAL = [(188, 198, 202), (156, 166, 170), (122, 130, 128), (52, 60, 56), (38, 46, 44),
       (78, 88, 80), (182, 178, 164), (118, 112, 102), (104, 106, 100), (86, 88, 84),
       (206, 214, 216)]
c = Canvas(44, 54, SKY)
# hill (right and bottom): straight pieces
c.poly([(40, 14), (43, 13), (43, 53), (12, 53), (16, 48), (22, 46), (24, 40), (26, 36),
        (30, 35), (33, 31), (38, 24), (40, 20)], HILL)
c.poly([(14, 53), (17, 49), (23, 47), (28, 47), (29, 53)], HILLD)
# crown: two masses joined, cut by straight pieces
c.poly([(17, 3), (22, 2), (26, 4), (29, 3), (33, 3), (38, 5), (41, 9), (41, 14), (39, 18),
        (40, 22), (37, 27), (33, 31), (31, 29), (30, 20), (28, 18), (24, 18), (22, 20),
        (21, 27), (19, 32), (16, 35), (10, 35), (6, 33), (5, 29), (6, 24), (5, 20), (8, 17),
        (12, 13), (15, 11), (16, 7)], DARK)
# core: strongest colour in the middle of the large masses (rule 4)
c.poly([(19, 6), (27, 7), (34, 7), (38, 11), (36, 16), (29, 15), (22, 14), (15, 16), (12, 20),
        (9, 26), (12, 31), (16, 28), (18, 20)], CORE)
# lit tops of the clumps (grey sky light from above: upper planes lighter, p. 114)
for x, y, n in [(18, 4, 4), (23, 3, 3), (30, 4, 5), (35, 6, 3), (13, 12, 3), (9, 17, 3),
                (31, 18, 2), (37, 19, 2), (6, 24, 2)]:
    c.hrun(x, y, n, LIT)
# the central bay: open sky under the crown, framed by limbs (the biggest aperture)
# holes: (x, y, shape) -- shapes as small row lists; 'o' open sky, 'v' veiled, 'c' globe centre
H = {
    'g2': ["oo", "oo"],
    'g3': [".o.", "oco", ".o."],
    'g4': [".oo.", "occo", ".oo."],
    'v3': ["vv.", "vvv", ".v."],
    'v4': [".vv.", "vvvv", ".vv."],
    'e5': [".oo..", "ocoo.", ".oooo", "..oo."],
}
for key, x, y in [('g3', 27, 11), ('g2', 24, 14), ('g4', 32, 15), ('g2', 36, 17), ('v3', 29, 8),
                  ('g3', 19, 18), ('e5', 11, 22), ('g3', 16, 23), ('g2', 8, 20), ('v4', 13, 26),
                  ('g2', 7, 28), ('v3', 20, 9), ('g3', 34, 22), ('v3', 15, 30)]:
    for j, row in enumerate(H[key]):
        for i, ch in enumerate(row):
            if ch != '.':
                c.px(x + i, y + j, {'o': SKY, 'c': SKYC, 'v': VEIL}[ch])
# rims: every foliage pixel 4-adjacent to a hole or the bay takes the colourless rim (rule 1),
# but only on the lower/left edges of holes -- where the upper foliage projects in front the
# edge stays sharp and dark (rule 5: the upper edge sharp explains the branch above).
a = c.a
foli = np.isin(a, [DARK, CORE, LIT])
hole = np.isin(a, [SKY, VEIL, SKYC])
inside = np.zeros_like(foli)
import scipy.ndimage as ndi
filled = ndi.binary_fill_holes(foli | np.isin(a, [VEIL, SKYC]) | ndi.binary_fill_holes(foli))
for y in range(1, 53):
    for x in range(1, 43):
        if foli[y, x]:
            below = hole[y + 1, x]; left = hole[y, x - 1]; right = hole[y, x + 1]
            if (below or left or right) and not hole[y - 1, x]:
                a[y, x] = RIM
# limbs, placed pixel by pixel (lit left, shadow right; sun from the left)
def limb(pts, w):
    for (x, y) in pts:
        for k in range(w):
            a[y, x + k] = WOOD if k < w - 1 or w == 1 else WOODD
trunk = [(28, 53), (28, 52), (28, 51), (27, 50), (27, 49), (27, 48), (26, 47), (26, 46), (26, 45),
         (25, 44), (25, 43), (25, 42), (24, 41), (24, 40), (24, 39), (23, 38), (23, 37), (23, 36),
         (23, 35), (22, 34)]
limb(trunk, 3)
left = [(21, 33), (20, 32), (19, 31), (18, 30), (17, 29), (16, 28), (15, 27), (14, 27), (13, 26),
        (12, 25), (11, 24), (10, 23)]
limb(left, 2)
mid = [(22, 33), (22, 32), (22, 31), (22, 30), (22, 29), (22, 28), (22, 27), (22, 26), (22, 25),
       (22, 24), (22, 23), (21, 22), (21, 21), (21, 20)]
limb(mid, 1)   # crossing the bright bay: thin (p. 45)
right = [(24, 33), (25, 32), (26, 31), (27, 30), (27, 29), (28, 28), (28, 27), (29, 26), (29, 25),
         (29, 24), (30, 23), (30, 22), (30, 21), (31, 20), (31, 19)]
limb(right, 1)
for p in [(17, 22), (16, 21), (15, 20), (26, 24), (25, 23), (24, 22), (32, 18), (33, 17)]:
    a[p[1], p[0]] = WOOD
ref = Image.open('../copies/ref_fig40.png').convert('RGB').resize((44 * 5, 54 * 5))
prev = Image.open('../copies/fig40_v1.png')
sheet([ref, c.img(PAL, 5), c.img(PAL, 1).resize((44 * 2, 54 * 2), Image.NEAREST)], 3,
      labels=['Cole fig. 40', 'hand copy 44x54 at 5x', 'at 2x']).save('../copies/fig40_v2.png')

"""Cole figs. 65-66 (p. 93): a tree line whose margin is built of straight pieces carried from
clump to clump. Copied by hand at 96x48 (landscape scale, ~0.6 m/px for trees ~20 m high).
The vertices are my choice of the 'builder's frame' (fig. 66's heavy lines)."""
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from px import Canvas
from lib import sheet

SKY, MASS, SHADE, RIM, TRUNK, GROUND, REED, WATER = range(8)
PAL = [(200, 206, 204), (86, 94, 84), (66, 74, 66), (128, 136, 128), (150, 150, 136),
       (176, 178, 162), (60, 64, 56), (188, 194, 192)]
c = Canvas(96, 48, SKY)
V = [(0, 6), (14, 6), (20, 7), (24, 9), (27, 12), (27, 16), (25, 18), (26, 21), (25, 24), (28, 26),
     (30, 23), (34, 21), (39, 20), (42, 18), (47, 18), (51, 19), (54, 21), (57, 24), (60, 24),
     (70, 24), (72, 21), (74, 17), (76, 15), (81, 15), (83, 17), (84, 21), (86, 25), (87, 26),
     (88, 21), (91, 17), (95, 16), (95, 36), (0, 36)]
c.poly(V, MASS)
# shade side of the separate crowns (sun from the left): a second tone of the same mass,
# from the same end of the scale (fig. 9) -- kept to the right-hand third of each crown
for poly in [[(18, 7), (24, 9), (27, 12), (27, 16), (25, 18), (26, 21), (25, 24), (20, 26)],
             [(47, 18), (51, 19), (54, 21), (57, 24), (52, 30), (48, 26)],
             [(81, 15), (83, 17), (84, 21), (86, 25), (82, 30), (80, 22)]]:
    c.poly(poly, SHADE)
a = c.a
# the big hole in the left tree, with the stems seen through it (fig. 65)
hole = ["..oooo.oo", ".ooooooooo", "oooooooooo.", ".ooooooo..", "..ooo...."]
for j, row in enumerate(hole):
    for i, ch in enumerate(row):
        if ch == 'o':
            a[21 + j, 11 + i] = SKY
for (x, y) in [(13, 21), (13, 22), (13, 23), (14, 24), (16, 22), (16, 23), (17, 24), (15, 25)]:
    a[y, x] = MASS                   # stems crossing the hole: dark, thin
# small holes: rims colourless, shapes varied (never stamped)
for pts in [[(41, 21), (42, 21), (42, 22)], [(77, 20), (78, 20), (78, 21), (79, 21)],
            [(90, 21), (91, 22), (90, 22)], [(62, 28)], [(33, 25), (34, 25)], [(46, 23)]]:
    for (x, y) in pts:
        a[y, x] = SKY
for y in range(1, 47):
    for x in range(1, 95):
        if a[y, x] in (MASS, SHADE) and (a[y + 1, x] == SKY or a[y, x - 1] == SKY) and a[y - 1, x] != SKY and y > 8:
            a[y, x] = RIM
# stems of the middle group, lit against the dark under-crown (Cole: trunks tell against foliage)
for x0, top, lean in [(35, 27, 0), (37, 29, 1), (44, 25, 0), (47, 27, -1), (58, 29, 0), (82, 30, 0)]:
    x = x0
    for y in range(top, 36):
        a[y, x] = TRUNK
        if lean and (y - top) % 4 == 3:
            x += lean
# ground: flat contact, a darker line under the mass (the contact line), then light field
a[36, :] = SHADE
a[37:, :] = GROUND
for x in range(0, 96):
    if (x * 7) % 11 < 3:
        a[37, x] = MASS
# water strip with reflections (fig. 65 has a pond): lighter band, broken dashes
a[38:40, 20:70] = WATER
for x in range(22, 68, 5):
    a[38 + (x % 2), x:x + 2] = RIM
# reeds: clustered vertical strokes of 2-5 px, uneven gaps (never evenly spaced, fig. 20-21)

Bash: cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat > copy_holly.py <<'E' """Cole Illus. 60 (Holly, p. 152), copied at 60x68. Clumps are placed by hand off the plate (list below). Each
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat > copy_holly.py <<'E'
"""Cole Illus. 60 (Holly, p. 152), copied at 60x68.
Clumps are placed by hand off the plate (list below). Each clump is painted with Cole's planes
(p. 114): its upper surface faces the grey sky and takes the light tone, the body the middle,
the underside and inner recess the dark; the whole dome adds a second, slower gradient (the
tree's planes: left/top light, right/bottom dark). Only adjacent tones meet. Edges: sprays
break the outline on the lit side, the shadow side is kept closer (p. 71: edges lose colour)."""
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from px import Canvas
from lib import sheet

SKY, EDGE, T1, T2, T3, T4, WOOD, WOODD, GRD, GRDD = range(10)
PAL = [(206, 206, 196), (160, 162, 150), (132, 136, 122), (100, 106, 94), (72, 78, 68),
       (46, 52, 46), (120, 108, 92), (70, 62, 54), (168, 162, 140), (130, 124, 104)]
W, H = 60, 68
# (cx, cy, rx, ry) -- read off the plate's value map, top to bottom
CLUMPS = [
    (27, 5, 2, 2), (26, 8, 3, 2), (28, 10, 4, 2), (24, 12, 3, 2), (31, 12, 4, 2), (35, 12, 3, 2),
    (20, 15, 4, 2), (27, 15, 5, 3), (34, 16, 4, 2), (39, 15, 2, 1),
    (18, 19, 4, 2), (25, 19, 5, 3), (32, 20, 5, 3), (37, 20, 3, 2),
    (12, 24, 5, 2), (19, 24, 4, 3), (27, 24, 5, 3), (34, 24, 5, 3), (40, 23, 3, 2),
    (10, 29, 4, 2), (17, 29, 5, 3), (25, 29, 5, 3), (33, 29, 6, 3), (41, 29, 5, 3), (47, 31, 4, 2),
    (14, 34, 5, 2), (22, 34, 5, 3), (30, 34, 6, 3), (38, 33, 6, 3), (45, 34, 5, 3), (50, 33, 3, 2),
    (6, 41, 5, 2), (13, 40, 5, 3), (21, 40, 6, 3), (30, 40, 6, 3), (39, 39, 6, 3), (47, 39, 5, 3),
    (3, 46, 4, 2), (10, 45, 5, 3), (18, 45, 5, 3), (27, 45, 7, 3), (37, 45, 6, 3), (46, 45, 5, 3),
    (5, 50, 4, 2), (14, 50, 5, 3), (24, 50, 6, 3), (34, 50, 7, 3), (44, 50, 6, 3), (51, 49, 3, 2),
    (17, 55, 5, 2), (27, 55, 7, 3), (37, 55, 7, 3), (46, 54, 4, 2),
]
rng = np.random.default_rng(7)
tone = np.full((H, W), -1.0)
yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
for (cx, cy, rx, ry) in CLUMPS:
    d = ((xx - cx) / (rx + 0.5)) ** 2 + ((yy - cy) / (ry + 0.5)) ** 2
    # ragged edge: sprays -- a clump's edge is broken by 1-px notches and jutting tips
    jag = rng.uniform(-0.35, 0.35, size=(H, W))
    inside = d + jag * (d > 0.5) < 1.0
    # plane within the clump: top rows light (faces the sky), underside dark
    v = (yy - (cy - ry)) / (2 * ry + 1)          # 0 top .. 1 bottom
    plane = np.clip(v, 0, 1)
    # dome gradient: the tree as one dome -- lighter up/left, darker down/right
    dome = 0.45 * (yy - 5) / 55 + 0.35 * (xx - 8) / 45
    t = 0.55 * plane + dome
    tone = np.where(inside, t, tone)            # later (lower) clumps overlap earlier
c = Canvas(W, H, SKY)
fol = tone >= 0
q = np.digitize(tone, [0.2, 0.42, 0.62])        # 0..3 -> T1..T4
c.a[fol] = (T1 + q)[fol]
# edges facing sky lose colour: outline pixels on the left/top take EDGE (colourless rim)
a = c.a
for y in range(1, H - 1):
    for x in range(1, W - 1):
        if fol[y, x] and (not fol[y, x - 1] or not fol[y - 1, x]) and x < 34:
            a[y, x] = EDGE if a[y, x] in (T1, T2) else a[y, x]
# stems: several trunks from one stool (the holly's multiple stems), pale on the left
for x0, top, lean in [(24, 50, 0), (27, 49, 1), (30, 51, 0), (33, 50, -1)]:
    x = x0
    for y in range(top, 61):
        if a[y, x] in (T3, T4) or y > 56:
            a[y, x] = WOOD
            a[y, x + 1] = WOODD
        if lean and (y - top) % 5 == 4:
            x += lean
# ground: the bank, flat contact, a dark pocket under the boughs
a[60:, :] = GRD
a[60, 14:52] = GRDD
for x in range(20, 44):
    if x % 3: a[61, x] = GRDD
ref = Image.open('../copies/ref_holly.png').convert('RGB').resize((W * 5, H * 5))
sheet([ref, c.img(PAL, 5), c.img(PAL, 2)], 3, labels=['Cole Illus. 60 Holly', 'copy 60x68 5x', '2x']).save('../copies/holly_v1.png')
E

Bash: cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat > copy_holly2.py <<'E' """Cole Illus. 60 (Holly) -- v2: box-downsampled plate as the underpainting (the studio's 'paint from the pho
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat > copy_holly2.py <<'E'
"""Cole Illus. 60 (Holly) -- v2: box-downsampled plate as the underpainting (the studio's
'paint from the photo at game res' method), then Cole's decisions applied pixel-class by class:

 1. four tones only, from one end of the scale for the shadow mass (fig. 9), the light tones
    reserved for the upper planes of clumps (p. 114);
 2. sky holes: plate-light pixels inside the crown kept as sky, pinholes (1 px) closed,
    each hole's lower/side rim colourless (p. 71);
 3. outline: foliage/sky boundary cleaned of single-pixel noise (no 'gimped edge', fig. 10), but
    spray tips kept on the lit left side;
 4. stems and ground drawn by hand."""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from PIL import Image
from px import Canvas, guide
from lib import sheet
from copy_holly import PAL, SKY, EDGE, T1, T2, T3, T4, WOOD, WOODD, GRD, GRDD, W, H

g = guide('../copies/ref_holly.png', W, H, show=False)
v = g * 10
yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
crown = (v >= 2.2) & (yy < 60)
crown = ndi.binary_opening(crown, structure=np.ones((2, 1))) | (crown & ndi.binary_erosion(crown))
lab, n = ndi.label(crown)
big = np.argmax(ndi.sum(crown, lab, range(1, n + 1))) + 1
crown = lab == big                                   # one tree
hull = ndi.binary_fill_holes(ndi.binary_closing(crown, iterations=2))
holes = hull & ~crown
hl, hn = ndi.label(holes)
for k in range(1, hn + 1):
    if (hl == k).sum() <= 1:
        crown |= hl == k                             # pinholes close
holes = hull & ~crown
a = np.full((H, W), SKY, np.uint8)
# tones: plate value -> 4 tones; smooth first so marks are clumps not static
vs = ndi.gaussian_filter(v, 0.6)
q = np.digitize(vs, [3.6, 5.2, 6.8])
a[crown] = (T1 + q)[crown]
# rims of holes and the outer edge on the lit side
rim = crown & ~ndi.binary_erosion(crown) & (xx < 32)
a[rim & np.isin(a, [T1, T2, T3])] = EDGE
rimh = ndi.binary_dilation(holes) & crown & ~ndi.binary_dilation(holes, structure=[[0, 0, 0], [0, 1, 0], [0, 1, 0]])
a[ndi.binary_dilation(holes) & crown & np.isin(a, [T1, T2])] = EDGE
# stems by hand: four stems from the stool, dark with a lit left edge, seen under the boughs
for x0, top in [(20, 55), (23, 53), (27, 54), (31, 55)]:
    for y in range(top, 61):
        a[y, x0] = WOOD; a[y, x0 + 1] = WOODD
a[60:, :] = GRD
for x in range(8, 58):
    a[60, x] = GRDD if (x % 4) else GRD
for x, y in [(3, 62), (4, 62), (40, 61), (41, 61), (42, 62), (50, 61), (51, 61), (52, 61), (55, 62)]:
    a[y, x] = GRDD
c = Canvas(W, H); c.a = a
ref = Image.open('../copies/ref_holly.png').convert('RGB').resize((W * 5, H * 5))
sheet([ref, c.img(PAL, 5), c.img(PAL, 2)], 3, labels=['Cole Illus. 60', 'copy v2 5x', '2x']).save('../copies/holly_v2.png')
np.save('../copies/holly_v2.npy', a)
E
python3 copy_holly2.py

"""The shelf painter: a grown tree (arch.py) painted the way Cole teaches, in pixels.

Unit of painting = one bough's spray shelf (Cole figs. 108-110: a side branch's branchlets lie in
one flat plane; Hallé: plagiotropic, dorsiventral). Each shelf is rasterized as columns under its
bough line; its pixels know where they sit in the shelf (top row / body / underside / tip).

Tones (index steps per material):
  FOL 0 deepest recess, 1 underside/shadow, 2 body, 3 sky-lit upper plane      (the grey-sky set)
  FOL 4 sun set low, 5 sun set high                                              (the sun set)
  FOL 6 colourless rim (edges and hole margins, Cole p. 71)
Wood 0..3: shadow, core, half, lit.
A pixel in a shelf's top row takes the upper-plane tone (3) -- Cole's planes of the dome, and the
conifer student's tier-top light; if the sun reaches it (not screened by a shelf above along
the sun direction) and it faces the sun, it takes the sun set instead (4/5) -- Cole's 'new and
lighter set, quite apart from the others'. Far-side shelves (behind the trunk) drop a step and
never take the sun set: they are seen through the crown's own shade.
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi

FOL, WOOD, SKY = 1, 2, 0


def _h(*a):
    """Deterministic hash -> [0,1) for per-pixel/per-shelf dithers (no stamped randomness)."""
    x = 0
    for v in a:
        x = (x * 1000003 + int(v)) & 0xFFFFFFFF
    x ^= x >> 13; x = (x * 0x5bd1e995) & 0xFFFFFFFF; x ^= x >> 15
    return (x & 0xFFFF) / 65536.0


def project(p, yaw, mpp, ox, oy):
    c, s = np.cos(yaw), np.sin(yaw)
    x = p[..., 0] * c - p[..., 1] * s
    dep = p[..., 0] * s + p[..., 1] * c
    return ox + x / mpp, oy - p[..., 2] / mpp, dep


def paint(tree, mpp, W, H, ox=None, oy=None, yaw=0.0, sun_side=-1, sun_el=40, seed=0,
          shelf_scale=1.0, droop_px=None, rim=True, sunset=True, far_step=True):
    sp = tree.sp
    ox = W / 2 if ox is None else ox
    oy = H - 2 if oy is None else oy
    mat = np.zeros((H, W), np.uint8)
    step = np.zeros((H, W), np.uint8)
    zb = np.full((H, W), -1e9)
    sid = np.full((H, W), -1, np.int32)
    vpos = np.zeros((H, W), np.float32)    # 0 top .. 1 bottom within a shelf
    far = np.zeros((H, W), bool)
    # --- trunk(s) and bare wood first (painted, then covered by nearer foliage) ---
    for p0, p1, r, o, dead in tree.segments():
        if o > 1 and not dead:
            continue
        (x0, y0, d0), (x1, y1, d1) = [project(np.asarray(p, float), yaw, mpp, ox, oy) for p in (p0, p1)]
        rp = r / mpp
        if rp < 0.25:
            continue
        n = int(max(abs(x1 - x0), abs(y1 - y0)) * 2) + 1
        for t in np.linspace(0, 1, n):
            cx, cy, dz = x0 + t * (x1 - x0), y0 + t * (y1 - y0), d0 + t * (d1 - d0)
            w = max(1, int(round(2 * rp)))
            xa = int(round(cx - w / 2))
            iy = int(round(cy))
            if not 0 <= iy < H:
                continue
            for k in range(w):
                ix = xa + k
                if 0 <= ix < W and dz >= zb[iy, ix] - 0.5:
                    # cylinder: 4 classes across the width, lit toward the sun
                    f = (k + 0.5) / w
                    f = f if sun_side < 0 else 1 - f
                    cls = 3 if f < 0.3 else (2 if f < 0.55 else (1 if f < 0.85 else 0))
                    if w == 1:
                        cls = 1
                    if w == 2:
                        cls = 2 if k == (0 if sun_side < 0 else 1) else 0
                    mat[iy, ix] = WOOD; step[iy, ix] = cls; zb[iy, ix] = dz
    # --- shelves ---
    trunk_axes = [i for i, a in enumerate(tree.axes) if a.order == 0 or a.reiterated]
    tx = np.mean([project(np.asarray(tree.axes[trunk_axes[0]].pts[0]), yaw, mpp, ox, oy)[0]])
    shelves = []
    for i, a in enumerate(tree.axes):
        if a.dead or len(a.pts) < 2:
            continue
        pts = np.array(tree.nodded(i, a.pts), float)
        ages = np.array(a.age_nodes[:len(pts)])
        X, Y, D = project(pts, yaw, mpp, ox, oy)
        if a.order == 0 or a.reiterated:
            # leader: last few years carry a spire of short shelves (the young top)
            young = ages >= tree.year - 3
            for k in np.where(young)[0]:
                shelves.append((i, X[k], Y[k], D[k], 1.0, 1.0, 0, 'tip'))
            continue
        for k in range(1, len(pts)):
            age = tree.year - ages[k]
            if age > (sp.spray_years or sp.needle_years):
                continue
            shelves.append((i, X[k], Y[k], D[k], X[k] - X[k - 1], Y[k] - Y[k - 1], age, 'bough'))
    # paint back to front
    shelves.sort(key=lambda s: s[3])
    reach_px = max(1.0, sp.reach * shelf_scale / mpp)
    th0 = max(1.0, sp.pad_thick / mpp)
    for (i, x, y, dz, dx, dy, age, kind) in shelves:
        if kind == 'tip':
            hw, th = max(1, int(round(reach_px * 0.3))), max(1, int(round(th0)))
        else:
            grow = min(1.0, (age + 1) / 6.0)
            hw = max(1, int(round(reach_px * grow)))            # wedge: narrow at the tip
            th = max(1, int(round(th0 * min(3.0, 0.6 + age / 8.0))))
        L = np.hypot(dx, dy) + 1e-9
        ux, uy = dx / L, dy / L                                # along the bough on screen
        # the shelf is a band along the bough segment, thick downward (sprays hang)
        span = int(np.ceil(max(abs(dx), 1))) + 1
        for s_ in range(-hw if kind == 'tip' else -int(max(1, abs(dx))), hw + 1 if kind == 'tip' else 1):
            cx = x + (s_ if kind == 'tip' else s_ * np.sign(dx) * 0 + s_ * ux)
            cy = y + (0 if kind == 'tip' else s_ * uy)
            # lateral fan: sprays spread sideways in the bough plane -> in side view they add
            # thickness below (fan edges droop) and a little width
            extra = int(_h(i, s_, 7, seed) * 2.0 + (sp.fan_droop * hw * 0.5 if kind == 'bough' else 0))
            ix = int(round(cx)); top = int(round(cy)) - (1 if th > 2 else 0)
            for yy in range(top, top + th + extra):
                if not (0 <= ix < W and 0 <= yy < H):
                    continue
                if dz >= zb[yy, ix] - 0.3 or mat[yy, ix] == 0:
                    mat[yy, ix] = FOL; zb[yy, ix] = dz; sid[yy, ix] = i
                    vpos[yy, ix] = (yy - top) / max(1, th + extra - 1)
                    far[yy, ix] = dz < -0.5 * sp.reach
    # --- tone the foliage ---
    F = mat == FOL
    up_same = np.zeros_like(F)
    up_same[1:] = F[:-1] & (sid[:-1] == sid[1:])
    top_row = F & ~up_same
    below_same = np.zeros_like(F)
    below_same[:-1] = F[1:] & (sid[1:] == sid[:-1])
    bottom_row = F & ~below_same
    # sun screening: march toward the sun (up and to sun_side) a few px; foliage of another
    # shelf there screens this pixel
    kx = -1 if sun_side < 0 else 1
    ky = -1
    screened = np.zeros_like(F)
    n = max(2, int(3 / np.tan(np.radians(max(10, sun_el))) + 1))
    for k in range(1, n + 1):
        sx, sy = int(round(k * kx / np.tan(np.radians(sun_el)) * 0.6)), k * ky
        sh = np.zeros_like(F)
        ys, xs = slice(max(0, -sy), H - max(0, sy)), slice(max(0, -sx), W - max(0, sx))
        yd, xd = slice(max(0, sy), H - max(0, -sy)), slice(max(0, sx), W - max(0, -sx))
        sh[ys, xs] = F[yd, xd] & (sid[yd, xd] != sid[ys, xs])
        screened |= sh
    tone = np.full((H, W), 2, np.uint8)
    tone[bottom_row] = 1
    tone[F & (vpos > 0.75)] = 1
    tone[top_row] = 3
    # sun-facing side of the crown: which side of the trunk
    xs_ = np.arange(W)[None, :].repeat(H, 0)
    sunward = (xs_ - tx) * sun_side > -2
    if sunset:
        tone[top_row & ~screened & sunward] = 5
        tone[top_row & ~screened & ~sunward] = 4
        # second row under a sun-lit top: the low sun tone on the sunward side only
        sec = np.zeros_like(F); sec[1:] = top_row[:-1] & ~screened[:-1]
        tone[sec & F & sunward & (tone == 2)] = 4
    # recesses: body pixels screened and far-side -> deepest
    tone[F & screened & (tone == 2) & ~sunward] = 1
    if far_step:
        tone[F & far & (tone >= 2)] -= 1
        tone[F & far & (tone >= 4)] = 2
    # rims: foliage pixels whose neighbour toward the sky (up or sunward side) is open sky take
    # the colourless rim -- only where they would otherwise be mid/dark body (p. 71)
    if rim:
        open_ = mat == SKY
        nb = np.zeros_like(F)
        nb[1:] |= open_[:-1]
        nb[:, 1:] |= open_[:, :-1] if sun_side < 0 else False
        nb[:, :-1] |= open_[:, 1:] if sun_side > 0 else False
        tone[F & nb & (tone <= 2) & ~top_row] = 6
    step[F] = tone[F]
    return mat, step


def palette(fol=None, wood=None, sky=((120, 170, 214), (190, 214, 230))):
    fol = fol or [(12, 22, 20), (24, 38, 32), (38, 56, 44), (62, 82, 64),
                  (104, 104, 50), (150, 144, 70), (96, 116, 116)]
    wood = wood or [(34, 24, 20), (58, 42, 34), (88, 66, 52), (124, 98, 76)]
    return dict(fol=fol, wood=wood, sky=sky)


def to_rgb(mat, step, pal):
    H, W = mat.shape
    top, bot = np.array(pal['sky'][0]), np.array(pal['sky'][1])
    t = np.linspace(0, 1, H)[:, None, None]
    out = np.round(top * (1 - t) + bot * t).astype(np.uint8).repeat(W, 1)
    for k, c in enumerate(pal['fol']):
        out[(mat == FOL) & (step == k)] = c
    for k, c in enumerate(pal['wood']):
        out[(mat == WOOD) & (step == k)] = c
    return out

def setp(block,key,val):
    return re.sub(r'(%s=)[-0-9.]+'%key, r'\g<1>%s'%val, block, count=1)

SP = {
    'douglas': Species('douglas-fir', model='massart', height=70, h_rate=0.9, h_tau=110,

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""imgfetch: fast, polite reference-image sourcing (Wikimedia Commons + Openverse).

  python3 imgfetch.py "cinder cone scoria slope" -n 12 -o refs/cinder
  python3 imgfetch.py -f queries.txt -n 8 -o refs            # one query per line; subdir per query
  options: --src commons,openverse   --width 1200   --sheet (writes <out>/_sheet.jpg)

Writes images plus <out>/sources.tsv (file, title, license, author, page url).
Cached: files already present are skipped. Handles 429/5xx with Retry-After backoff.
Parallel downloads (4 workers) with a shared per-host rate limiter (~3 req/s; Wikimedia's media
host is kept to 1 per 2 s and slows further on any 429, since it throttles hard per IP after bursts).
Openverse (mostly Flickr-hosted CC images) is the primary source because it stays fast.
"""
import argparse, concurrent.futures as cf, json, os, re, sys, threading, time, urllib.error, urllib.parse, urllib.request

UA = "pixelart-studies-imgfetch/1.0 (research; contact: local)"
_locks = {}; _last = {}; _glock = threading.Lock()

SLOW_HOSTS = {"upload.wikimedia.org": 2.0}   # Wikimedia media host: ~1 req/s or it 429s

def _throttle(host, min_gap=0.34):
    min_gap = SLOW_HOSTS.get(host, min_gap)
    with _glock:
        lk = _locks.setdefault(host, threading.Lock())
    with lk:
        dt = time.time() - _last.get(host, 0)
        if dt < min_gap:
            time.sleep(min_gap - dt)
        _last[host] = time.time()

def get(url, tries=4, timeout=25):
    host = urllib.parse.urlparse(url).netloc
    if host == "upload.wikimedia.org":
        tries = 2  # fail fast; other sources fill in
    for i in range(tries):
        _throttle(host)
        try:
            req = urllib.request.Request(url, headers={"User-Agent": UA})
            return urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout).read()
        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:

Write: books/trees/LESSONS.md
# Cole and Hallé–Oldeman in pixels: lessons

A student's log, kept honestly as it goes. The book's model in its own terms is in MODEL.md.
Code in `src/`, master copies in `copies/`, work-in-progress in `work/`, final sheets in `sheets/`.

## 0. Reading (done first)

Read Cole whole (text + every page as contact sheets, key figures at 220 dpi) and Hallé's
chapters 2–5 (text, the illustrated key, the model descriptions for the temperate genera,
reiteration, the forest chapter). MODEL.md holds the result with page references.

The first thing the reading changed: I had expected Cole to be about *drawing trees* and Hallé
about *growing* them. Cole's Part II turned out to be a naive architecture — bud arrangement →
branch plan → outline — and Hallé's reiteration chapter turned out to be the missing account of
what Cole calls "the closeness of multiplied forms" of old trees. They are one model.

## 1. First mistake: I built a renderer before I could draw

I read, then wrote a growth simulator (`arch.py`, Hallé's models as programs) and a generic
painter (`cole.py`: splat foliage, dome shading, straightened outline). The first renders
(`work/first.png`, `work/dbg.png`) were exactly what the coordinator warned of from the Genge
study: generic lumps, Cole's rules applied to a blob that had none of his drawing in it. The
dome shading gave a smooth two-tone cone with a vertical terminator — the thing the conifer
student's critic had already killed. I stopped and went to master copies.

## 2. Master copies

Rule for myself: place pixels deliberately, at small size, against the book's own figure;
compare at 3–5×; repeat until the copy carries what the figure carries.

### 2a. Hallé's key, Massart and Troll (`copies/massart_v1.png`, `copies/troll_v1.png`)

Drawn with `src/copy_halle.py`: every branch is a list of straight runs (`arc(runs, dys)`),
which is Cole's p. 92 made literal — *"the comparative length of these straight pieces
determine the character of the curve."* A Massart branch rising and flattening is runs
1,2,2,3,4,5,6 stepping up; the oldest tier's droop is runs 4,7,10 up-level then 5,2,1 down.
Hallé's hanging leaf loops became a 2-px drop under every other branch pixel.

What carried: the four-stage sequence on one soil line *is* the model — the same tree read as
architectures in time (Hallé p. 75). The tier spacing, the trunk thickening below the second
tier, the young top with spiral leaves. Troll's arches (up, crest, fall) read at 1×.
What didn't: at this size the loops are a comb; Hallé's loops are the only place his drawing is
decorative, and pixel combs are the studio's known failure ("barcode"). They are not needed —
the arch alone says plagiotropic.

### 2b. Cole fig. 40, the sky apertures (`copies/fig40_v1..v4.png`)

v1 (automatic: plate thresholded, rules applied by code) was mush. v2 (polygons + stamped
holes) had "+"-shaped holes — the studio's *stamped ellipse* failure. v3–v4 are typed as ASCII
rows against the plate's value map (`src/copy_fig40c.py`), with Cole's legend: sky, veiled sky,
hole centre, colourless rim, foliage, core, lit plane, wood lit/shadow, hill.

What the copy taught, by pixels:

- **The colourless rim is one pixel.** A mid grey between the dark mass and the sky, on the
  lower and side margins of each hole (not the top: rule 5 — where the upper foliage projects,
  the edge is sharp). At 2× that single grey pixel makes the holes read as light *hanging among
  the leaves* rather than cut-outs. This is the most transferable thing in Chapter IV.
- **Holes are never symmetric.** They elongate along the lines between clumps and link into
  chains (p. 80); a 2×2 or plus-shaped hole is a stamp.
- **Veiled holes one step darker than sky** carry depth for free.
- Still wrong in v4: the plate's pale limbs cross *over* the dark mass ("against the veil the
  exquisite lines of the larger boughs can be clearly traced", p. 78); mine stop at the crown.

### 2c. Cole figs. 65–66, a tree line of straight pieces (`copies/fig65_v1..v2.png`)

Landscape scale, 96×48, one flat mass (Cole p. 51: massed trees, one depth of colour). The
outline is a polygon whose vertices I chose — the "builder's frame" of fig. 66. v1 had long
axis-aligned runs (a flat top on the big tree) and read as cut cardboard; v2 uses short angled
pieces with small bites, and reads as a tree line at 2×. The shade tone is kept to a 2–3 px band
on each crown's right flank, from the same end of the scale as the mass (fig. 9).
Honest gap: the plate's clumps have more small secondary notches than mine.

### 2d. Cole Illus. 60, Holly (`copies/holly_v1..v2.png`)

A conifer-like cone at close scale, the nearest thing in Cole to a young Douglas-fir or spruce.
v1 placed ~50 clumps by hand, each painted with Cole's planes (top light, body, underside dark)
plus a dome gradient: the result was a *striped cake* — regular ellipses, no holes, the value
bands in rows. v2 used the studio's method (box-downsample the plate to game resolution and paint
from that) and then applied the rules class by class: four tones, pinholes closed, colourless
rims on the lit side, hand-drawn stems and bank. v2 carries the form at 2× but is still blotchy:
the plate's marks are strokes following hanging sprays, and a downsample cannot give strokes.

from lib import *
import shelf, time, sys
from PIL import Image
mpp=0.4
names=sys.argv[1].split(',') if len(sys.argv)>1 else ['douglas','hemlock','cedar','spruce']
ages=[15,40,90,250]
cells=[];labs=[]
Hmax=int(80/mpp); Wc=int(34/mpp)
for n in names:
    for a in ages:
        t0=time.time(); t=grow(n,a,seed=3,open_grown=False)
        mat,step=shelf.paint(t,mpp,Wc,Hmax)
        cells.append(shelf.to_rgb(mat,step,shelf.palette())); labs.append(f'{n} {a} yr  {t.height():.0f} m  ({time.time()-t0:.0f}s)')
        print(labs[-1], flush=True)
sheet([up(c,2) for c in cells],len(ages),labels=labs,title='species x age (Hallé sequence), forest-grown, 0.4 m/px, 2x').save('../work/ages_v1.png')

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/trees/src; cat > study_oak_winter.py <<'E'
"""Study: a Garry oak in winter, after Cole p. 78 (leafless trees) and ch. VI (the oak's elbows),
from the photo refs/Quercus_garryana_tree/openverse_Oregon_s_Oak_Tree. 100x100 px, ~0.22 m/px.

Cole p. 78: 'The border is constructed of little twigs ... a haze scarcely differing from the
colour of the sky. Inside this border is a closer network ... an indistinct veil, and against it
the exquisite lines of the larger boughs can be clearly traced.'
Cole p. 157 / Hallé Rauh: oak shoots at near right angles, sturdy, elbowed; supple, the leader
replaced again and again, so the crown is a set of reiterated limbs, no straight trunk above
the fork.
The limbs are traced by hand (lists of points read off the photo at game resolution) and
drawn as clean runs; the telescope rule (Cole fig. 155) steps their width down at each fork.
The haze and veil are zones by distance from the limb network inside a hand-cut hull."""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
from px import Canvas
from lib import sheet, up

SKY0, SKY1, HAZE, VEIL, NET, BARK, BARKL, BARKD, GRD, GRDD, BG, BGD = range(12)
PAL = [(150, 186, 222), (168, 198, 228), (136, 158, 176), (108, 116, 118), (80, 80, 78),
       (62, 56, 50), (104, 96, 84), (40, 36, 34), (116, 104, 80), (92, 82, 64), (56, 70, 52),
       (38, 50, 38)]
W, H = 100, 100
c = Canvas(W, H, SKY0)
a = c.a
a[:40][np.add.outer(np.arange(40), np.arange(W)) % 2 == 0] = SKY0
a[45:, :] = SKY1
for y in range(38, 46):                        # dithered seam between the two sky bands
    a[y, (np.arange(W) + y) % 2 == 0] = SKY1

# limbs: (points, width at start, width at end); elbows are written in as sharp turns
LIMBS = [
    ([(53, 99), (53, 90), (52, 80), (52, 70), (51, 62), (51, 59)], 5, 4),                 # trunk
    ([(51, 59), (44, 60), (37, 60), (33, 58), (28, 59), (23, 57), (18, 55), (14, 50), (10, 47), (7, 42)], 3, 1),  # A
    ([(50, 55), (47, 50), (43, 44), (38, 40), (33, 35), (30, 29), (24, 23), (22, 19), (20, 16)], 3, 1),          # B
    ([(52, 53), (52, 45), (51, 36), (48, 30), (50, 24), (49, 19), (46, 12), (46, 6)], 3, 1),                     # C
    ([(54, 52), (57, 46), (61, 38), (64, 33), (67, 26), (67, 20), (69, 13)], 2, 1),                             # D
    ([(55, 56), (61, 52), (69, 47), (75, 46), (82, 41), (88, 39), (93, 36)], 2, 1),                             # E
    ([(57, 59), (65, 60), (74, 59), (79, 55), (84, 54), (89, 50)], 2, 1),                                       # F
    ([(28, 59), (25, 54), (22, 50), (20, 45), (17, 41)], 1, 1),
    ([(18, 55), (14, 54), (10, 53), (6, 53)], 1, 1),
    ([(33, 35), (36, 30), (38, 26), (38, 21), (40, 15)], 1, 1),
    ([(24, 23), (27, 18), (27, 13), (30, 9)], 1, 1),
    ([(51, 36), (55, 31), (57, 28), (57, 22), (59, 17), (58, 11)], 1, 1),
    ([(61, 38), (66, 36), (72, 33), (76, 29), (80, 27)], 1, 1),
    ([(69, 47), (72, 42), (75, 36), (76, 30), (79, 25)], 1, 1),
    ([(43, 44), (40, 47), (35, 48), (30, 46), (26, 45)], 1, 1),
    ([(74, 59), (78, 62), (84, 62)], 1, 1),
    ([(46, 12), (42, 8), (39, 5)], 1, 1),
    ([(67, 20), (72, 16), (75, 12)], 1, 1),
]
net = np.zeros((H, W), bool)
def seg(p, q, w, col):
    x0, y0 = p; x1, y1 = q
    n = max(abs(x1 - x0), abs(y1 - y0)) + 1
    for t in np.linspace(0, 1, n):
        x = int(round(x0 + t * (x1 - x0))); y = int(round(y0 + t * (y1 - y0)))
        for k in range(w):
            xx = x + k - w // 2
            if 0 <= xx < W and 0 <= y < H:
                a[y, xx] = BARKL if (k == 0 and w > 2) else (BARKD if (k == w - 1 and w > 1) else col)
                net[y, xx] = True
for pts, w0, w1 in LIMBS:
    m = len(pts) - 1
    for k in range(m):
        w = int(round(w0 + (w1 - w0) * k / max(1, m - 1)))       # steps down along the limb
        seg(pts[k], pts[k + 1], w, BARK)

# the crown hull cut in straight pieces (Cole p. 92): the oak's border is shorter, more broken
# pieces than an elm's, with star-shaped projections
HULL = [(3, 42), (6, 33), (12, 25), (18, 14), (26, 8), (36, 3), (46, 1), (55, 2), (62, 5), (70, 7),
        (76, 12), (82, 18), (86, 26), (93, 30), (98, 36), (96, 42), (90, 50), (92, 56), (84, 60),
        (72, 64), (58, 63), (44, 64), (30, 63), (18, 60), (10, 56), (4, 50)]
hm = Image.new('L', (W, H), 0); ImageDraw.Draw(hm).polygon(HULL, fill=1)
hull = np.array(hm, bool)
d = ndi.distance_transform_edt(~net)
edge_d = ndi.distance_transform_edt(hull)
hsh = np.fromfunction(lambda y, x: (((x.astype(np.int64) * 92821) ^ (y.astype(np.int64) * 68917)) % 997) / 997.0, (H, W))
sky = ~net
# network: within 2 px of the limbs, dense veil; 2-5 px, veil half; outer: haze thin
zone_net = hull & sky & (d <= 1.5)
zone_veil = hull & sky & (d > 1.5) & (d <= 4.5)
zone_haze = hull & sky & (d > 4.5)
a[zone_net & (hsh < 0.75)] = NET
a[zone_net & (hsh >= 0.75)] = VEIL
a[zone_veil & (hsh < 0.55)] = VEIL
a[zone_veil & (hsh >= 0.55) & (hsh < 0.75)] = HAZE
a[zone_haze & (hsh < 0.45)] = HAZE
# the extreme border: haze thins to nothing over the last 2 px (no edge -- p. 78)
border = hull & (edge_d <= 2) & sky
a[border & (hsh < 0.7) & (a == HAZE)] = SKY1 if False else a[border & (hsh < 0.7) & (a == HAZE)]
a[border & (hsh >= 0.25) & np.isin(a, [HAZE, VEIL])] = SKY0
# background: the dark evergreen line behind, and the ground
bg = np.zeros((H, W), bool)
prof = [62, 60, 64, 58, 55, 57, 61, 63, 60, 62]
for x in range(W):
    top = prof[x // 10] + (1 if (x * 7) % 5 == 0 else 0)
    bg[top:88, x] = True
a[bg & ~net] = BG
a[bg & ~net & (hsh < 0.35)] = BGD
a[88:, :] = GRD
a[88, :] = GRDD
seg((53, 88), (53, 99), 5, BARK)
ref = Image.open('../tmp/oak_box.png').convert('RGB')
ph = Image.open('../tmp/oak_photo.jpg').crop((40, 100, 820, 880)).resize((500, 500))
sheet([ph, ref, c.img(PAL, 5)], 3, labels=['photo', 'photo at 100 px (box)', 'study v1, 5x']).save('../work/oak_winter_v1.png')
c.img(PAL, 1).save('../work/oak_winter_v1_1x.png')

s=s.replace("net = np.zeros((H, W), bool)","""def elbow(pts, amp=1.5, every=1):
    # Cole ch. VI: the oak's line is the elbow -- break each span into two pieces with a sharp
    # turn, alternating sides (a zigzag of short straight lengths, never a smooth curve)
    out = [pts[0]]
    for k in range(len(pts) - 1):
        (x0, y0), (x1, y1) = pts[k], pts[k + 1]
        mx, my = (x0 + x1) / 2, (y0 + y1) / 2
        nx, ny = -(y1 - y0), (x1 - x0)
        L = max(1e-6, (nx * nx + ny * ny) ** 0.5)
        sgn = 1 if k % 2 == 0 else -1
        out.append((int(round(mx + sgn * amp * nx / L)), int(round(my + sgn * amp * ny / L))))
        out.append((x1, y1))
    return out
LIMBS = [(p if i == 0 else elbow(p, 1.4 if w0 > 1 else 1.0), w0, w1) for i, (p, w0, w1) in enumerate(LIMBS)]
net = np.zeros((H, W), bool)""")
a0=s.index("# network: within 2 px"); b0=s.index("# background: the dark evergreen line behind")
s=s[:a0]+'''# twig clumps (the oak's tufts, Cole p. 66/158: 'tufted ... star-shaped projections'): centred on
# the limb ends and at intervals along the outer limbs; each clump a dense core of network,
# a veil ring, then haze -- so the crown is clusters with sky between, not an even stipple
ends = []
for pts, w0, w1 in LIMBS[1:]:
    ends.append(pts[-1])
    for q in pts[len(pts) // 2::3]:
        ends.append(q)
cl = np.zeros((H, W))
yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
for k, (ex, ey) in enumerate(ends):
    r = 2.5 + 2.5 * ((k * 37) % 7) / 7
    # star-shaped: radius modulated by angle (5 points)
    ang = np.arctan2(yy - ey, xx - ex)
    rr = r * (1 + 0.35 * np.cos(5 * ang + k))
    dd = np.hypot(xx - ex, yy - ey) / rr
    cl = np.maximum(cl, np.clip(1.2 - dd, 0, 1))
cl *= hull
zn = sky & (cl > 0.75); zv = sky & (cl > 0.45) & ~zn; zh = sky & (cl > 0.12) & ~zn & ~zv
a[zn & (hsh < 0.8)] = NET; a[zn & (hsh >= 0.8)] = VEIL
a[zv & (hsh < 0.6)] = VEIL; a[zv & (hsh >= 0.6) & (hsh < 0.8)] = HAZE
a[zh & (hsh < 0.4)] = HAZE
'''+s[b0:]
s=s.replace("oak_winter_v1","oak_winter_v2").replace("'study v1, 5x'","'study v2, 5x'")
open('study_oak_winter.py','w').write(s)

"""Cole p. 64-66: 'A distant tree, rendered flat in tone by the atmosphere, is recognised by the
pattern it makes against the sky ... Marked differences in the construction of the outline
become apparent.' Washington trees as far silhouettes, typed by hand at ~1.5 m/px.

planes (used only in the MID set), '.' = sky. Each silhouette states its species by the

def rows(s):
    r = [x for x in s.strip('\n').split('\n')]
    w = max(len(x) for x in r)
    return [x.ljust(w, '.') for x in r]


FAR = [(170, 182, 190), (116, 128, 132), (150, 160, 164)]   # sky, far tone, lit
cells, labs = [], []
for k, s in T.items():
    r = rows(s)
    c = Canvas(len(r[0]) + 4, 50, 0)
    c.stamp(r, 2, 50 - len(r) - 1, {'#': 1, '+': 2})
    cells.append(c.img(FAR, 4)); labs.append(k)
sheet(cells, 10, labels=labs, title="far silhouettes, hand-typed, ~1.5 m/px, 4x (Cole p. 64: species by outline alone)").save('../work/far_v1.png')
